### First Continuation of the Second Vision

One might therefore accuse the author of having told an untruth; yet how easy must it have been for him to speak an untruth, given the great multitude of images with which his head was filled? He clearly interprets these images as the promised return of his Master, the **Lion of Judah** <note>A biblical title for the Messiah, here interpreted by the author as an astronomical symbol</note>, since all these depictions—if one does not account for their astronomical meaning—contain nothing but untruths.

But that the **Lamb**, which took the book and broke its seals, was nothing more than the **Ram** <note>The constellation Aries</note> of the Zodiac, is evident from the entire description of it. This **Ram** certainly had the greatest right to this role, because it was precisely the celestial sign in which the seven gods, or the seven great <term>genii</term> <note>original: "Geniüsse"; in ancient belief, these were guardian spirits or divine intelligences associated with the planets</note> of the planets, were to assemble to hold a great council over their future constellations, and consequently over the future destinies of mankind.

But the many millions of angels who stood around the throne, the four beasts, and the elders, were the <term>genii</term> of the stars: for in ancient times, every star had its own particular genius.

As for the rest, regarding the heavenly music of the spheres <note>original: "Sphärenmusik"; the ancient philosophical concept that the movement of celestial bodies creates a divine, though inaudible, harmony</note>, or the prayers and songs of this entire heavenly host: it is certainly true that the stars, which taken together constitute this heavenly host, do indeed greatly glorify the divine majesty of the great Creator in the minds of men, whenever they occasionally turn their contemplations toward the admirable structure of the universe.

In the introduction, it was shown that the ancient stargazers <note>original: "Sternseher"; an early term for astronomers or astrologers</note> depicted the constant cycle of the stars, the end of which could not be foreseen, in the form of a coiled snake that ate its own tail <note>This refers to the Ouroboros, an ancient symbol of infinity and the cyclical nature of time</note>, and thus seemed to have neither beginning nor end. <meta>This page continues the discussion from page 66 regarding the symbolic representation of time. The previous page ended mid-word ("onl...") while describing why ancient artists might only depict the front half of a winged horse.</meta>

->Revelation VI. 1 — 14<-

...y the front part of the body, to indicate thereby that only one part of time has fleetingly passed, while the other is still in the future.

The ancient Egyptians used this image to show the fleeting nature of the nine months of their field work. Instead of a calendar, they placed a symbol in a public place for each moon <note>meaning each lunar month</note>, which indicated the specific tasks that fell within that month. To this, they likely also added the winged horse each time, seeking thereby to encourage the people to be industrious.

But the Greeks transformed these symbols of the nine working months into nine industrious maidens, the **nine Muses**; the aforementioned symbol of fleetingness, however, they turned into the **Muse-horse** <note>original: "Musenpferd"; referring to Pegasus, the winged horse of Greek mythology associated with poetic inspiration</note>. Finally, the symbols of the remaining three months—during which the Egyptians could not work because of the flooded Nile, but instead enjoyed themselves upon the hills—were transformed into three young dancing maidens, the **three Graces**.

Thus, the four horses in the Apocalypse are likely nothing more than symbols of four different periods of time. But why specifically a **chestnut**, a **white horse**, a **black horse**, and a **pale horse**? We wish to investigate the matter further.

It was shown extensively in the introduction that the division of the **Zodiac** <term>Zodiac: the "circle of animals" or belt of constellations through which the sun, moon, and planets appear to move</term> into twelve **constellations** was one of the first tasks of the ancient stargazers. It was also shown that the number of these images did not arise by chance, nor was it made larger or smaller, because the moon sets **heliacally** <term>heliacal setting: when a star or planet disappears into the sun's bright light at evening</term> twelve times a year near twelve different groups of stars. In the same way, I also proved there that those ancient stargazers, from whom these oldest...

The heaven folded itself up and vanished, as one rolls up a parchment skin, and all the mountains and islands were moved from their places.

In this, nothing further is obscure, except for the history of the four horses and their riders, which I must therefore explain in detail.

In the introduction, it was shown that the most ancient stargazers <note>original: "Sternseher"; referring to early astronomers or astrologers</note> depicted the constant cycle of the stars, the end of which was not to be foreseen, in the form of a serpent coiled together, eating its own tail in its gullet <note>this is the Ouroboros, a symbol of infinity and the cyclical nature of the universe</note>, and thus appearing to have neither beginning nor end. But this symbol did not suit the fleetness of time, which they also found necessary to represent in many cases. They therefore had to create a new one, and for this they chose a fast animal: a horse. To give it even more speed, they added a pair of wings to its shoulder blades and painted only the front part of it,

### Revelation VI. 1—14

...to indicate thereby that only a part of the time had fleetingly passed, while the other was still in the future.

The ancient Egyptians used this image to show the fleetness of the nine months spent in field labor. In place of a calendar, they would place a symbol for each moon <note>referring to a lunar month</note> in a public area, which indicated the specific tasks to be done during that month. To this, they likely also added the winged horse <note>this refers to the concept of time flying; the author links this to the origins of Pegasus</note>, seeking to encourage the people toward diligence in their work.

But the Greeks transformed these symbols of the nine working months into nine industrious young women, the nine Muses. The aforementioned symbol of fleetness, however, was turned into the "Muses’ Horse" <note>the winged horse Pegasus, who in myth created the spring of the Muses with his hoof</note>, and finally, the symbols for the remaining three months—during which the Egyptians could not work because the Nile had overflowed, and instead passed the time enjoying themselves on higher ground—were turned into three young dancing women: the three Graces.

Thus, the four horses in the Apocalypse <note>the Book of Revelation</note> are likely nothing more than the symbols for four different periods of time. But why exactly a sorrel <note>original: "Fuchs"; a chestnut or reddish-brown horse, representing the red horse of War</note>, a white horse <note>original: "Schimmel"; representing Conquest</note>, a black horse <note>original: "Rappen"; representing Famine</note>, and a pale horse <note>original: "Falbe"; a dun or yellowish-grey horse, representing Death</note>? We wish to investigate this matter further.

It was shown in detail in the introduction that the division of the zodiac into twelve constellations was among the first tasks of the ancient stargazers. It was also noted that the number of these images did not arise by chance; they were made neither more nor less because the moon sets heliacally <note><term>heliacal</term>: occurring when a celestial body rises or sets at the same time as the sun, making it briefly visible in the dawn or twilight</note> twelve times a year alongside twelve different groups of stars. ### Second Continuation of the Second Vision

...[from our] oldest astronomical works originate, lived under a considerable latitude of our temperate earth-belt.

So they now had twelve signs of the zodiac <term>Zodiac: the "circle of animals," a belt of the heavens divided into twelve signs</term>. What was then more natural than that they should likewise divide the remaining stars—which appeared to them almost all above the zodiac in the northern hemisphere of the sky—into twelve groups, in order to establish, as it were, a certain symmetry across the heavens? These twelve groups were undoubtedly the Great Bear, the Little Bear, the Dragon <note>the constellation Draco</note>, Boötes, the Crown <note>Corona Borealis</note>, Hercules, the Swan <note>Cygnus</note>, the Charioteer <note>the constellation Auriga</note>, Andromeda, Perseus, Cassiopeia, and Cepheus; however, as is easy to see, they did not yet bear these names at that time, but only received them long afterward from the Greeks.

But then people began to realize that the sky was not a vaulted roof, but rather like a hollow sphere, and consequently probably also contained twelve constellations below the zodiac, though for the most part these never rose above the horizon.

In this way, those twenty-four constellations arose, of which I have said that the Chaldeans <note>an ancient Mesopotamian people renowned for their early mastery of astronomy</note> and Persians regarded them as the twenty-four judges of the dead and the living, or as the twenty-four Elders of Heaven <note>referring to the 24 Elders mentioned in the Book of Revelation</note>. Previously, however, before they were appointed as judges, the upper twelve ruled the day, which was therefore divided into twelve equal parts, subjecting one such part to each. The lower twelve, on the other hand, ruled the night, which was likewise divided into twelve equal parts, so that one such part could be subjected to each. From these twice-twelve parts, which naturally were sometimes longer and sometimes shorter <note>the author refers to "seasonal hours," where the length of an hour changed based on the amount of daylight available throughout the year</note>,

->### Revelation VI. 1—14<-

...[were], depending on how the length of the day or the night varied according to the different seasons; thus arose the two-times-twelve unequal hours of the natural day, which were still in fashion among the Jews even until the times of their dispersion <note>original: "Zerstreuung"; referring to the Jewish Diaspora</note>. More clever and industrious peoples, however, <insert>abolished</insert> these unequal hours quite soon, by counting the night with the day and dividing both taken together into twenty-four consistently equal parts, which were measured on clear days with sundials <note>literally "sun-pointers"</note>, but in cloudy weather or at night with water clocks <term>Water clock: also known as a clepsydra, an ancient device that measured time by the regulated flow of water from one container to another</term>.

Through this improvement of the hours, however, their regents—the two-times-twelve stars—gradually lost their domains. For at that time, one by no means dared to count them consecutively up to twenty-four, and thus to mix the kings of the underworld with the princes of the upper world <note>the author refers to the ancient belief that constellations below the horizon ruled the "underworld" while those above ruled the "upper world"</note>; yet this would have had to happen if the hours were to continue to be ruled by them. Therefore, people preferred to have these [hours] ruled by the planets, specifically in such a way that the first was given to Saturn, the second to Jupiter, the third to Mars, the fourth to the Sun, the fifth to Mercury, the sixth to Venus, the seventh to the Moon, the eighth again to Saturn, the ninth again to Jupiter, and so on, continuing through all hours and all days in the same sequence until the first hour fell once again to Saturn, and thus the week was completed. It should only be noted here that the planets followed one another in the exact same order in which the Chaldeans <note>ancient Mesopotamian astronomers</note> and East Indians at that time arranged them according to their distance from above down toward the Earth: namely <term>♄ ♃ ♂ ☉ ☿ ♀ ☽: the astrological symbols for Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, the Sun, Mercury, Venus, and the Moon</term>, that is, **Saturn**, **Jupiter**, **Mars**, **Sun**, **Mercury**, **Venus**, and **Moon**.

### Second Continuation of the Second Vision
->catchword: hour<-

Thus, if one allows the planets to rule the hours one after another in the order mentioned: the same planet that falls on the first hour will also fall on the eighth, fifteenth, and twenty-second hours every time, and this planet always gives the day its name, as may be clarified by the attached diagram.

<image-desc>
An astrological diagram representing the twenty-four hours of the day and their planetary rulers across the week. At the top is a horizontal row of twenty-four planetary symbols in the Chaldaean order <note>The "Chaldaean order" arranges the planets by their perceived orbital speed/distance from Earth in ancient geocentric astronomy: Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon.</note>: Saturn (♄), Jupiter (♃), Mars (♂), Sun (☉), Venus (♀), Mercury (☿), and Moon (☽), repeated to fill the line. 
Below this is a grid with rows corresponding to the days of the week, labeled on the left with symbols for the Sun (☉), Moon (☽), Mars (♂), Mercury (☿), Venus (♀), and Jupiter (♃). Each row contains dots aligned with the columns, with the day's ruler repeated at specific intervals (representing the 1st, 8th, 15th, and 22nd hours). At the end of each row on the right is the symbol of the planet ruling the 24th hour of that day. 
A final row of planetary symbols runs along the bottom of the diagram. The layout demonstrates how the 24-hour cycle shifts the ruler of the first hour to the next day's namesake.
</image-desc>

Here, the horizontal lines contain the twenty-four hours, while the vertical columns from the top down contain the seven days.

Saturn, being the uppermost <note>In the geocentric model, Saturn was considered the "highest" or outermost planet.</note>, began and ruled the first, eighth, fifteenth, and twenty-second hours of the first day; this day was therefore called **Saturday** <note>original: "Samstag"</note>, because the day was always given the name of the planet to which the rule of its first hour fell. Since the twenty-fourth hour of the first day was the second hour after the twenty-second, and Mars was the second planet after Saturn <note>in the descending Chaldaean order: Saturn (22), Jupiter (23), Mars (24)</note>: the rule of this last hour fell to Mars. Consequently, the first hour of the second day fell to the planet that followed immediately after Mars, which was the Sun; the Sun thus gave the name to the second day, that is, **Sunday**. This planet now likewise ruled the eighth, fifteenth, and twenty-second hours of this day: consequently, the rule of the twenty-fourth fell to Venus, and thus the rule of the first hour of the third day fell to the Moon, which was therefore called **Monday**.

<summary>This page explains the astrological origins of the names of the days of the week, detailing how the sequence of planetary rulers for each of the twenty-four hours results in a new planet governing the start of each subsequent day.</summary>
<keywords>planetary hours, Chaldaean order, astrology, days of the week, Saturn, Sun, Moon, Saturday, Sunday, Monday, timekeeping</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the mathematical demonstration of the planetary hours. The previous page established that the first hour of the first day (Saturday) was ruled by Saturn. By counting through the 24 hours of each day using the "Chaldaean order" (Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon), the ruler of the first hour of each subsequent day changes, thus dictating the names of the days of the week.</meta>

### Revelation 6:1–14

The [twenty-fourth] hour of that same day fell to **Jupiter**, who was followed by **Mars**; he therefore ruled the first hour of the fourth day, and thus bestowed upon it the name of **Mars-day**. <note>The author is explaining the origin of the names of the days of the week; Tuesday is *dies Martis* in Latin or *mardi* in French.</note> The second planet after **Mars** was **Mercury**, followed by **Venus**: the former ruled the last hour of the fourth day, while the latter ruled the first of the fifth, which consequently received the name of **Venus-day**. <note>Friday, or *dies Veneris* / *vendredi*.</note> The last hour of the fifth day fell, according to the order, to **Saturn**, and consequently the first of the sixth to **Jupiter**, who therefore gave this day the name of **Jove’s day**. <note>Thursday, or *dies Iovis* / *jeudi*. "Jove" is an alternative name for the god and planet Jupiter.</note> On this day, the **Sun** ruled in the last hour, which was followed immediately by **Mercury**: he thus received the governance of the seventh day, and therefore gave it the name of **Mercury-day**. <note>Wednesday, or *dies Mercurii* / *mercredi*.</note> The last hour of this day fell to the **Moon**: consequently, the first hour of the eighth day fell again to **Saturn**, who thus began the week anew.

According to this arrangement, the names of the days followed one another in this fashion: **Saturn’s day**, **Sun-day**, **Moon-day**, **Mars-day**, **Venus-day**, **Jove’s day**, and **Mercury-day**.

From the aforementioned order of the planets, one can see that the oldest astronomers in **East India** and **Babylon** estimated the distance of **Mercury** from the Earth to be greater than the distance of **Venus**: but the **Egyptians** reversed the matter and placed **Venus** further away from us than **Mercury**. Both parties were sometimes right and sometimes wrong in this regard, and could therefore easily waver back and forth between this or that opinion: for **Venus** is, as is well known, often truly much closer to us than **Mercury**, just as the latter is often much closer to us than **Venus**. One would think that this small astronomical dispute would have changed nothing in the world—

# Revelation VI. 1 — 14

...flowed from the sun as it were upon the earth, since one made the sun the supreme God: and now it would probably have been sinful if one had further robbed him of this honor, and had not wanted to make his day the first of the week. In short, Sunday now became the first, and thus Saturn-day the last, day of the week, and all seven followed one another according to the latter schema in the following manner: **Sunday**, **Monday**, **Mars-day**, **Mercury-day**, **Jove-day**, **Venus-day**, **Saturn-day**.

The ancient Germans, our ancestors, revered **Mars** under the name of **Dien** <note>Dien refers to the Germanic god Tiw or Tyr, from whom we get "Tuesday".</note>, **Mercury** under the name of **Wodan**, **Jupiter** under the name of the great **Thor**, and finally **Venus** under the name of **Freia**: consequently our **Sunday** is the day of the Sun; **Monday**, the day of the **Moon**; **Tuesday**, the day of **Dien** or of **Mars**; **Wednesday** <note>original: "Mittewoch" (Mid-week)</note>, which only in some Lower Saxon provinces is still called **Woens-** or **Wodan's Day**, is the day of **Wodan**, or of **Mercury**; **Thursday** or **Thor's Day**, the day of the great **Thor**, or of **Jupiter**; **Friday**, the day of **Freia** or of **Venus**, and finally **Saturday** <note>original: "Sonnabend" (Sun-eve)</note>, which however only the Swabians and Franks quite correctly call **Saturn's-day** or **Samstag**, the day of **Saturn**. Yet this latter remark concerning the German names of the seven days of the week was only made in passing, and is not intended to help contribute anything to the achievement of our purpose.

But this is well to be considered: that the ancient **priests** <note>original: "Sacerdoten"</note>, who alone were in possession of the sciences of that time, now also dedicated the known seven metals to the seven planets, in that the Sun was **Gold**, the Moon was **Silver**, **Mars** was **Iron**, **Venus** was **Copper** or **Ore**, **Jupiter** was **Tin**,

Scorpio, were taken, and of which each was intended to last three millennia; thus, for the entire duration of the world, exactly as many thousand years emerged as there were constellations of the Zodiac, just as it is written in the Zend-Avesta <note>The primary collection of religious texts of Zoroastrianism.</note>. Hesiod <note>An ancient Greek poet (c. 700 BC); the author refers to his "Ages of Man" from *Works and Days*.</note> does indeed let the brazen age follow immediately upon his silver race of men, and places his iron age last, while inserting a race of heroes or demigods between the two: but one must forgive a poet for such small historical inaccuracies.

Accordingly, our apocalyptic **sorrel** <note>original: "Fuchs," literally a fox-colored or chestnut horse; corresponds to the "red horse" of Revelation 6:4.</note> signifies the golden age; the **white horse** <note>original: "Schimmel"</note> the silver; the **black horse** <note>original: "Rappen"</note> the iron; and finally the **pale horse** <note>original: "Falbe"</note> the brazen <note>or bronze</note> world age. It is clear that either the author himself, or his copyists, or his sources, have quite erroneously placed the **white horse** before the **sorrel**. However, that the images of fleeting times in ancient times were truly represented as horses, I have already sufficiently proven.

Thus, the first man, who received a crown and falsely rode a **white horse** (because the **sorrel** belonged to him), represented the Genius <note>A guardian spirit or personification of a particular quality or era.</note> of that golden race of men of the first world age, which according to the myth was happy on earth, like gods, and conquered all the discomforts of life because none could harm them.

# Revelation VI. 1—14

...mankind: and for this reason, the spirit <note>original: "Genius," referring to the personified guiding spirit of a particular era.</note> of this age of the world was given the power to kill a fourth part with swords, with hunger, with disease, and with all kinds of poisonous pests. However, he was given exactly a fourth part of the world and exactly four ways of murdering specifically because he was the spirit of the fourth age of the world, and because in earlier times the whole world was divided into four parts in order to assign one quarter to each primary celestial body. It follows, then, that the fourth part which the spirit had the power to kill here was the fourth quarter of the world.

Previously, we saw that the two-times-twelve elders—who ruled the two-times-twelve parts of the natural day (the black and white hours, the dead and the living, or day and night)—were toppled from their thrones and stripped of their dominion by wicked astronomers who then divided the day into twenty-four parts of equal size. We have further seen that even later astrologers in Babylon even dared to place foreign deities—the planets of the Egyptians—alongside the ancient supreme gods (the four most prominent constellations of the Zodiac <note>original: "Thierkreis," literally "animal circle."</note>). Indeed, they even assigned them dominion over the hours of the day, the days of the week, and the ages of the world. This gave rise to discontent, discord, and hatred between the neologists <note>A term used here for innovators or those introducing "new" ideas into established systems.</note> and the orthodox-minded astrologers. The latter primarily presided over divine worship at that time and were extraordinarily indignant over these godless disturbances of the gods as well as of the laws of time. If the <term>ages of the world<gloss>original: "Weltalter"; the concept that human history is divided into distinct epochs characterized by different qualities</gloss></term> had been set at seven from the very beginning, and thus taken from the seven classical planets: then the poets would certainly have also added the tin, mercury, and leaden world-ages to the golden, silver, iron, and bronze ones. However, this certainly could not be easily done, and it is clear why they mostly had to satisfy themselves with the above four.

**Hesiod** <note>Hesiod (c. 700 BCE) was an ancient Greek poet whose work "Works and Days" first popularized the concept of the Ages of Man.</note> indeed counts, as mentioned, five kinds of human generations: yet one also sees very clearly that the fourth, or the **generation of heroes**, evidently belongs to the preceding iron one, and was only cut off from it because of the particular mythology of the Greeks.

From the image, which had a golden head, a silver chest, a bronze belly, and a pair of iron thighs—and of which King **Nebuchadnezzar** is said to have dreamed according to the report of **Daniel**, who explained it through four great kingdoms—it is likewise evident that in high antiquity the duration of the world was divided into only four parts. These same four world-ages were also seen by **Zechariah** in the form of four large chariots driving out from between bronze mountains, except that he confused them even a bit more than **Daniel** and **Hesiod**, by having the first drawn quite correctly by chestnut <note>original: "Füchsen," literally "foxes," used here to describe sorrel or reddish-gold horses.</note> or golden steeds, the second however by black horses, the third by white horses, and the fourth by dappled ones. — Could these four ages of the world perhaps even have been derived from the four seasons?

The four corners of the earth signify the four cardinal directions: for the earth is <term>spheroidal<gloss>original: "sphäroidisch"</gloss></term> <note>The author is pointing out a perceived scientific error in the biblical text, noting that a spherical earth cannot have literal corners.</note>, and does not have four corners. But the four angels, whom the author saw standing at these four cardinal points, were the four ancient images of the gods of the wind, whom—according to the delusion of many ancient nations—they occasionally depicted with puffed-out cheeks, blowing storms from all four cardinal points over the earth and the sea. Since now winter and summer, frost and heat, thirst and hunger, thunder and storm wind, toil and labor, had to cease at the end of the sixth age of the world—according to <term>Jewish poetic traditions<gloss>original: "jüdischen Dichtungen"</gloss></term>—so that the great Sabbath of the Lord could begin: therefore the storm winds were no longer permitted to damage the seas, the lands, and the trees, but had to sit quite still throughout the Sabbath.

# Revelation VI. 1—14
F 2

...[high]est <note>original: "sten", likely concluding the word "höchsten" (highest)</note> Imperial Seal-Bearer of the living God, because he once viewed the **Ram**, or the **Lamb**, as **Jesus**, who, as said, had reliably promised to establish the Jewish hierarchy and to soon commence his reign.

One should consider here that those **astrologers** who declared the **constellations** of the **zodiac** to be the supreme gods, and began counting them from **Taurus** <note>original: "Stiere"</note>, allowed the last of them—namely the **Ram**, or the **Lamb**—to reach the **throne** at the end of the current **world**. Meanwhile, those who made only the **planets** into the supreme gods assigned the government in the final age of the world to either **Saturn** or the **Moon**. It is clearly seen that our author <note>Referring to the author of Revelation, traditionally John of Patmos</note> has melted both opinions into a single one and, out of a misunderstanding, interpreted them as the reign of his master, whom he expected at any hour.

Regarding the twelve times twelve thousand of the sealed: the astrological sense of this expression meant nothing more than that the stars of the twelve constellations of the zodiac should not perish at the end of the world, but rather be preserved until the future world. This was because the seven geniuses of the planets would then have to recreate all their constellations anew, for which the stars of these twelve constellations would, of course, be absolutely necessary. According to the **Mithraic mysteries** <term>Mithraic mysteries<gloss>original: "Mithrageheimnisse"</gloss></term>, they were even supposed to take over the government of the four world ages again, which had once been snatched from them by the planets. Consequently, there was a twofold reason why they could not perish entirely. But for our author, these twelve constellations had to indicate the twelve tribes of his nation, while the individual stars indicated the twelve times twelve thousand men of these twelve tribes. ### Third Continuation of the Second Vision

As for the sealing, the author seems to have taken this from **Nehemiah** <note>Referring to the biblical book of Nehemiah, specifically chapters 9 and 10, where the leaders of Israel set their seal to a renewed covenant with God</note>.

What else could his Jewish pride find in these twelve constellations besides the twelve sons of **Jacob**, since **Joseph’s** dream <note>A reference to Genesis 37:9, where Joseph dreams of the sun, moon, and eleven stars—representing his family—bowing down to him</note> taught this so tangibly? What else could the individual stars of these constellations be to him but **Jews**?

The fact that exactly twelve thousand stars were assigned to each constellation came about because they wished to suggest that each contained an uncountably large number of stars. They played with the number twelve, just as such games with other numbers were very fashionable among **astrologers** at all times, especially with the numbers **three** and **seven**. The former because of the three persons of procreation <note>A likely reference to the ancient concept of the triad: father, mother, and child as a fundamental unit of existence</note>, and the latter because of the seven **planets**.

But the great multitudes who had gathered from all kinds of nations and stood before the Lamb to serve him were the **geniuses** <term>geniuses<gloss>original: "Geniusse"</gloss></term> <note>In ancient and Enlightenment-era thought, a 'genius' (from the Latin genius) referred to a tutelary spirit or divine essence governing a person, place, or, in this case, a celestial body</note> of the remaining stars of heaven. These could not, of course, bear the twelve names of the twelve tribes of Israel, and consequently had to be **Gentiles** <note>original: "Heiden" (Heathens/Pagans)</note>. They were clothed in white garments because the stars have always shone white, and were thus dressed in white.

One might object that the author could not well have used these **geniuses** of the stars—those located outside the **zodiac** <note>original: "Thierkreisse"</note>—for a second time, as they were already contained within the twenty-four **elders**, who after all signified nothing more than the twice-twelve **geniuses** of the constellations placed outside the **zodiac** by the **ancients**. However, one must not look for such fine **accuracies** <note>original: "Akkuratessen"</note> in his works:

### Revelation 8:1–4, 9–17

...to seek: he was glad whenever he found quite many images, so that he might be able to artificially weave them into his system regarding the then-imminent return and universal reign of his Master, even if they happened to represent the same thing twice. Moreover, the **geniuses** <term>geniuses<gloss>original: "Geniusse"</gloss></term> <note>As established previously, these are the presiding spirits of the stars</note> of individual stars were truly distinct from the **geniuses** of entire constellations: and for this reason, the author’s blinded imagination could all the more easily regard both types as two different classes of the chosen: the twice-twelve judges, and the gathered peoples of all kinds of languages.

Since the twenty-four elders, as well as those great multitudes, signified the stars outside of the zodiac, they were actually the very same ones who, at the opening of the fifth seal, sat under the altar <note>A reference to Revelation 6:9, where the souls of the martyrs are seen under the altar</note> and were consequently still in great tribulation, from which they have just now returned after the breaking of the sixth seal. Furthermore, that these **geniuses** will no longer feel either thirst or hunger, and that neither heat nor frost can harm them further, will be easily conceded without my reminding; just as I probably need not first say that the author's words regarding the fountain of living water, the tears that God shall wipe from the eyes of these **star-spirits** <note>original: "Sterngeister"</note>, and the seals given to the chosen on their foreheads, actually mean nothing more than—

### Third Continuation of the Second Vision

The act of sealing seems, moreover, to have been taken by the author from Nehemiah <note>Likely referring to Nehemiah 9:38 or 10:1, where the leaders of Israel "seal" a written covenant.</note>.

What else could his Jewish pride find in these twelve constellations <note>The twelve signs of the Zodiac</note> but the twelve sons of Jacob, since **Joseph’s dream** so obviously taught this? What else could the individual stars within these constellations be to him but Jews?

The fact that exactly twelve thousand stars were assigned to each constellation <note>Referring to the 12,000 from each of the 12 tribes in Revelation 7:4–8</note> arose because he wished to suggest that each contained an uncountably large number of stars, and because he was playing with the number twelve. Such **numerological toys** <note>original: "Spielwerke"</note> involving other numbers were very much in fashion among **astrologers** <term>astrologers<gloss>original: "Astrologen"</gloss></term> at all times, especially with the numbers three and seven. The former was favored because of the **three persons of procreation** <note>A reference to the triad of father, mother, and child as a natural mystery</note>, and the latter because of the **seven planets** <note>In ancient astronomy, the "seven planets" included the Sun and Moon alongside Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn</note>.

But the great multitudes that had gathered from all kinds of nations and stood before the **Lamb** to serve him were the **geniuses** <term>geniuses<gloss>original: "Geniusse"</gloss></term> <note>The presiding spirits or souls of the celestial bodies</note> of the remaining stars in the sky. These, of course, could not bear the twelve names of the twelve **Jewish tribes**, and consequently, they had to be **Gentiles**. They were clothed in white garments because the stars have always shone white, and were therefore "dressed" in white.

Perhaps one might object that the author could not well use these **geniuses** of the stars—those located outside the **zodiac** <note>original: "Thierkreiß"</note>—for a second time, as they were already included among the **twenty-four elders**. Those elders, after all, signified nothing more than the two-times-twelve **geniuses** of the constellations that the ancients placed outside the zodiac. However, one must not seek such **fine accuracy** <note>original: "feine Akkuratessen"</note> in his works.

...smashed all the ships. This *star* was called *Wormwood* <note>Wormwood (*Artemisia absinthium*) is a plant known for its extreme bitterness; here it symbolizes the poisoning of life-sustaining waters</note>: therefore the *rivers* and *springs* upon which it fell became *wormwood*, and many *people* died from these bitter *waters*. Now the fourth *angel* blew his trumpet: and quickly a *third* of the *sun*, a *third* of the *moon*, and a *third* of the *stars* was extinguished, so that not only a *third* of the *night*, but also a *third* of the *day*, was lost. At the opening of the preceding *seal*, that is, at the beginning of the last *age of the world*, our *author*, as we have seen, let the dawn of the great *Sabbath* <term>Sabbath<gloss>original: "Sabbath"</gloss></term> <note>In this theological context, the "Sabbath" refers to the final age of rest or the Millennial Kingdom after the world's "week" of six thousand years</note> be announced through various *signs in heaven*, and had it immediately celebrated by the *chosen* before the *throne* of the *Lamb*. But now the last *seal* was opened. Consequently, the great *Sabbath*, or the last *age* of the *world*, had to end now; that is, the last great *Day of Judgment* had to break in with power.

Since the *author* was undisputedly a born *Jew*: various Jewish *ceremonies* certainly had to be observed at the dawn of the same, for otherwise *God* might perhaps have become incensed, and in his fierce *zeal* <note>The German *Eifer* translates here as "zeal" or "wrath," reflecting the Old Testament description of a "jealous God" who demands strict adherence to ritual and law</note>, as the *prophets* along with *Moses* depict him, trampled everything under his *feet*, so that even the *author* himself might have [lost] his *little place* in the new *Jerusalem*...

# Third Vision

...could have come. Thus the high priest <term>high priest<gloss>Hohepriester</gloss></term>, who appeared here in the form of an angel, had to burn incense on the golden altar as a sweet aroma before the Lord at the opening of the final seal, and then have the conclusion of the Sabbath announced to the city, as was customary, by several Levites in the form of angels with trumpets. That exactly seven such angels blew their trumpets arose from the fact that the ancient astronomers <note>original: "Sterndeuter," meaning those who interpreted the stars; the author suggests the writer of Revelation was influenced by pagan science</note>, from whom our author had learned his art of prophecy, assigned seven lesser servant spirits to the seven supreme gods—the planets. These spirits then blew a short fanfare in honor of their masters, each individually, in the exact order in which their masters were once arranged in Egypt in ancient times, according to their distance from the Earth.

Just as many calendar makers even now assign to each planet dominion over certain plants, animals, countries, rivers, and so forth, for the sake of maintaining superstition, so too did the ancient astrologers dedicate to each planet a certain class of creatures. These, as is well known, consisted in antiquity of three different principles: an airy, a watery, and an earthy principle—or of **spirit**, **soul**, and **body**. The spiritual or airy principle came from the **Father** of natural products, the **sun**; the watery or lunar <note>original: "selenitische," derived from the Greek moon goddess Selene</note> principle from the **Mother**, the **moon**; and finally the earthy or terrestrial principle from nutrients. Thus, the first two had a heavenly origin—that is, they were spiritual emanations of the two great lights of the world, or their guiding spirits <term>guiding spirits<gloss>Geniuse</gloss></term>—and could therefore not be destroyed, but had to last eternally. But the third part <note>A reference to the "third" of the earth/sea/trees destroyed in the previous biblical verses</note> was certainly subject to decay or corruption: and for this reason, it could indeed always

Finally, it must also be remembered that an old astrological fable <note>The author uses "Mährchen" (modern: Märchen) to suggest that these ancient beliefs were mythological or superstitious rather than divine truth.</note> claimed the second world would be destroyed by fire, just as it had long ago let the first be destroyed by water—that is, by a great flood.

When the **first angel** sounded his trumpet: the earthly third of the trees and plants was burned with a hail of blood and fire for this reason—because this trumpet was intended for the bloodthirsty **Saturn**, to whom hail, as well as the plant kingdom, was sacred.

Consequently, at the sound of the trumpet, a fiery mountain had to immediately plunge into the sea and burn not only the earthly third of all living creatures within it, but also the earthly third of all ships; for at that time, these also possessed a spiritual, a watery, and an earthly principle, and by fire, everything earthly simply had to be burned.

Without a doubt, this star represented nothing more than **Mars**, whose red glow resembles the light of a burning torch, and who is well known as the god of war. War, of course, tastes bitter to most people and slaughters many of them. The fourth trumpeter was the attendant of the sun, who was given the supreme oversight of light, and who immediately killed the earthly third part of the light of both the night and the day as her trumpeter sounded the trumpet in her honor.

Whenever the ancient <term>astrologers</term> <note>Scholars who studied the movements of stars to predict earthly events.</note> and <term>priests</term> <note>original: "Sacerdoten"; from the Latin 'sacerdos'.</note> painted a symbol of the earth, they also frequently added the seven planets to it, in such a way that usually four of them stood on one side and three on the other. In this way, people became accustomed to dividing the planets almost always into two different clusters—namely, the cluster on the right and the cluster on the left. For this reason, it happened that a great angel, the <term>Genius of the Earth</term> <note>A presiding spirit or guardian of the world.</note>, seemed to fly through the heavens to our author between these two clusters, causing a short pause in the trumpet concert. Because of the three angels who were still to sound their trumpets, he cried out his triple **Woe!** over the poor inhabitants of the earth—which, however, will not mean much, as we shall soon see.

In the introduction, we saw that later priests, out of simplicity and a penchant for the miraculous, reshaped that old symbol of the **heliacal** <term>heliacal: refers to the "heliacal setting," the last time a star or planet is visible in the evening sky before it is lost in the sun's glare.</term> setting of the morning star into a pictorial representation of the fall of a god who had previously been good. They then imagined of this fallen god that he—along with his servant spirits, the geniuses of the stars, with whom he had set heliacally and thus, as it were, fallen below the horizon—inhabited the lowest places of the earth, the dark **Orcus** <note>original: "Orkus"; the Roman name for the underworld or the god of the dead.</note>, the black hell, and from there, to the mockery of the good heavenly gods, brought forth all evil in the world. The earliest naturalists had once been led by the beneficial effects of the sun's warmth to the delusion of a spiritual, good outpouring of the stars, and so they let everything come directly from a good god. But their descendants simultaneously found much evil in the world; consequently, these people certainly required evil **demons** <note>original: "Dämonen"; derived from the Greek "daimon." While originally meaning a spirit or divine power, the author uses it here to describe the personification of evil forces.</note> who constantly rebelled against the good gods and corrupted everything that the former had made well. How? Could God have found all his creatures to be "very good," and then yet cursed the serpents out of revenge? Could creatures even be capable of offending the Creator, of enraging Him, or of feuding with Him? Might one not call this depiction of the deity nothing less than blasphemy?

But the star that the author saw falling from heaven was **Lucifer**, or the heliacally <term>heliacal: when a star or planet "sets heliacally," it disappears into the sun's brightness, which ancient observers often described as a "fall" from the sky.</term> setting morning star itself. To be sure, he was once—just as he still is—sometimes pushed down below the horizon by the rising sun at the eastern horizon; that is, according to the delusion of the astrologers, cast into the **well of the abyss** <note>original: "Brunnen des Abgrundes"; the bottomless pit described in Revelation 9:1-2.</note>, into the innermost regions of the earth, into the great fiery furnace. There, he supposedly unleashed his fury against gods and men with all his might, using volcanoes as muzzles through which he spewed steam and fire to darken the air and the sun.

What else could the fire-spewing mountains be to the author and his informants than the fire-ovens of hell? And how could these possibly spew fire if they were not unlocked by the fallen angel of the abyss?

In this way, all those evil demons generally arose, about which it was once firmly and stubbornly believed—both in the Jewish land <note>Judea</note> and in the rest of the Orient—that the entire atmosphere was filled with them, that all illnesses originated from them, and that humans were often led into evil by them. It was believed that under the leadership of the fallen **Morning Star**, their prince—whom the author calls **Abaddon** in Hebrew and **Apollyon**, the Destroyer, in Greek—they waged a constant war in the air against the good gods, and so forth.

This ancient astrological fairy tale <note>original: "Märchen"; the author uses this to suggest the stories are mythological rather than literal truth</note> has taken such deep root that even now, in many oriental countries and languages, all illnesses are called "evil spirits" and all patients are called "the possessed."

Because of the illnesses that these demons caused, they were given deadly scorpions’ tails. But since they flew about in the air, they also had to have wings and, consequently, a resemblance to voracious locusts. They were very strong and waged war with gods: therefore, they had to resemble the armored steeds of warriors. As great, albeit evil, heroes, they had to wear golden crowns and have human faces. They plunged people into ruin under the cloak of gentleness and love; consequently, their hair was like women's hair, and their teeth were like the teeth of lions. Of course, they also had to wear breastplates: for otherwise, they would have been wounded too often in battle.

# Revelation 9:1–19

These strange images should not seem at all alien to us: for one need only cast a glance at the hieroglyphs of the Egyptians, and one will find a multitude of similar monsters.

Following Venus, the later astronomers had Mercury follow. The astrologers also turned him into an evil spirit, the god of thieves, robbers, and usurers: for they likewise saw him sometimes setting **heliacally** <term>heliacally<gloss>The heliacal rising or setting of a planet occurs when it first becomes visible in the dawn or disappears in the twilight, appearing to stay close to the sun.</gloss></term> in the East, or falling down from heaven. They suspected, therefore, that the other gods—who never set heliacally in the East, but only in the West—occasionally chased him out of heaven, or at least sent him to earth and into hell as a messenger. Therefore, the sixth angel, whom our author heard trumpeting, was the servant of Mercury, who also blew a little piece on the trumpet in honor of his master, and thereby simultaneously announced his arrival in **Aries** <note>original: "Widder"</note> where, exactly now at the end of the last world age, all seven planets were to gather again, as I have already shown above.

Above we have seen that the four stars of the solstices and equinoxes were once cheated out of their dominion over the world ages by heterodox **priests** <note>original: "Sacerdoten"</note> and, along with those constellations of the northern and southern skies, were cast, as it were, under the table or altar. Therefore, now at the end of all things, the four **genii** <note>Protective spirits or personifications of the four points of the year.</note> of the same cried out with powerful voices to the trumpeter of the messenger of the gods from the four corners of the golden altar, giving the command to release the four angels who lay bound at the great river **Euphrates**!

<meta>This page continues the interpretation of Revelation 9, specifically focusing on the four angels bound at the river Euphrates. The author is moving from the planetary influence of Mercury into a broader astronomical explanation of the zodiac and celestial "rivers."</meta>

### First Continuation of the Third Vision

...still lie there, as if bound, as I will soon show.

Initially, the Milky Way of heaven was compared only to a great stream: but later this comparison was also extended to the **zodiac** <term>zodiac<gloss>The "circle of animals" or the belt of the heavens through which the sun, moon, and planets appear to move.</gloss></term>, because it was likewise seen in the shape of a wide band, or stream, flowing unstoppably from East to West across the heavens. In this way, in the minds of the **Chaldeans** <note>The Chaldeans were ancient inhabitants of Mesopotamia, widely regarded by historical scholars as the founders of astrology and astronomy.</note>, who knew no greater river than the Euphrates, there arose the idea of the great river where the aforementioned four gods were supposedly bound.

A twelfth part of the day passed whenever each of these **animals** <note>The constellations of the zodiac.</note> rose daily from beginning to end—that is, as it rose above the horizon. A whole day passed as each one circled the entire sky once from East to West. A **month** <note>original: "Mond" (moon). In this context, it refers to the lunar month.</note> passed as the moon completed its course once around the whole sky from West to East, and consequently passed through each of them. Finally, an entire year passed before the sun completed its annual path through each of them. Since a twelfth part of the day was also called an hour: they certainly had something to command over an hour, a day, a month, and a year. 

Therefore, at the end of the last **world age** <note>original: "Weltalter," referring to a great cycle of time in cosmic history.</note>, according to our author's concepts, they had to be released to be ready for an hour, a day, a month, and a year to kill the earthly third of mankind, because he [the author of Revelation] had once and for all decided to have those people killed who were not sealed Jews. However, so that these might suffer a truly shameful death, he had the many thousands of thousands of **horsemen** <note>original: "Reisigen," referring to the cavalry or mounted troops mentioned in Revelation 9:16.</note> of these four released angels armored with fiery sulfurous breastplates...

He heard seven such thunders because the number seven was once considered sacred due to the seven planets <note>In ancient astronomy, the "seven planets" included the Sun and Moon along with Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn.</note>, and because astrologers and soothsayers were accustomed to performing all sorts of hocus-pocus with that number.

By the fact that the author did not record the speeches of the seven thunders, we have certainly lost nothing, as they likely prophesied nothing more than the cruel tortures with which the "unsealed"—those who were not initiated into the ancient mysteries—were to be tormented at the end of the world. However, from the fact that a voice from heaven commanded him not to write them down, but rather to seal them, it seems to follow once more that he truly was initiated into some branch of the ancient mysteries, or at least caught wind of something from an initiate which he was not permitted to blab about. For the secret teachings of antiquity, which among the Egyptians and Chaldeans <note>The Chaldeans were a people of Mesopotamia famous in the ancient world for their knowledge of astronomy and astrology.</note> consisted for the most part only in the art of prophecy and natural magic, were indeed hidden under the veil of such hieroglyphs <note>The author uses "hieroglyphs" here in the sense of "mystical symbols" or "coded language" rather than specifically Egyptian writing.</note>. Be that as it may: one will hopefully now agree with me that all these images are nothing more than astrological symbols, and therefore contain no secrets, much less indicate future events. Instead, they must have been known in Egypt and Chaldea <note>The Chaldeans were a people of Mesopotamia famous in the ancient world for their knowledge of astronomy and astrology.</note> long before the author’s time, as is sufficiently clear from the books of Daniel and Ezekiel.

Now, finally, the seventh angel sounded his trumpet. By rights, several more great atmospheric phenomena <note>The author uses "Lufterscheinungen" (literally "air-appearances") to refer to meteorological events like lightning or storms.</note> should have appeared at this point, because this angel represented the servant or the trumpeter of the Moon, to whom dominion over the air was entrusted. Therefore, our author has once again somewhat violated the proper order of things here, letting those seven thunders roar a bit too early. They really should only have roared now, as the servant of this lowest planet <note>In the ancient geocentric model, the Moon was the "lowest" or closest celestial body to Earth, and was thought to influence the "sublunary" atmosphere.</note> blew into the trumpet. 

For the same reason, the angel who was clothed in clouds and had a rainbow around his head should only have appeared to the author now; these two attributes of the image of the world clearly indicated those <term>meteors: in this historical context, "meteors" refers to any atmospheric phenomenon (like rain, wind, or rainbows), not just falling rocks from space</term> over which the Moon was thought to rule. Instead, he suddenly has a song of triumph strike up in heaven. From its conclusion, one can clearly infer that the twenty-four elders were not blessed spirits, but merely the twice-twelve constellations of the northern and southern skies. In remote antiquity, these were regarded by the Chaldeans as the twenty-four judges of the living and the dead—that is, as the most ancient presiding spirits of the "black" and "white" hours of the day and night. Later, however, they were stripped of their dominion by the astronomers. According to the delusions of the weak-minded, who held them to be actual gods, they certainly...

### Revelation X. 1—7. XI. 14 — 19 ###

...whether these are right, I will not investigate now; but it is my heartfelt wish, because I am likewise of their opinion in this matter.

Thus, the author could certainly see his temple and his ark of the covenant in heaven: for he was undoubtedly a born Jew, and among the Jews these things were the most holy; indeed, it was impossible for them to seek things in heaven that contained more beauty, brighter radiance, more sublimity, and a greater mystery than the gilded ark of the covenant or the temple. In this, they thought just like the initiates to whom the <term>Hierophant: a priest in ancient Greece who interpreted sacred mysteries and esoteric knowledge</term> taught that one day the <term>Mysterien: sacred secret rites or mysteries</term> would be celebrated eternally in heaven.

Up to this point, our author has recounted the tales of the <term>Sterndeuter: astrologers or "star-interpreters"</term> regarding the seven ages of the world, the destruction of the world, and the <term>Wiederbringung aller Dinge: the "restoration of all things," a theological concept (apokatastasis) suggesting the eventual salvation of all souls</term> in reasonably good order; but now he begins to become confused in his presentation. Strictly speaking, he should proceed immediately from the temple of God, which he saw open in heaven, to the new Jerusalem, to the new heaven, and to the new earth: for the other visions he has recounted up to that point are nothing more than fragments, which partly fit here and there into the remaining gaps of the already constructed building, and partly appear merely for a second time, only in a different form, and could therefore easily be left out altogether. However, he had collected them once with great effort, without knowing that there were <term>Dubletten: duplicates or repetitions of the same theme</term> among them; and he was far too conscientious to withhold them from his readers.

Probably most of the images and tales he has described so far originally came from Egypt, whereas those he describes in the following...

# Revelation 10:8 — 13.
G 5

...[prophe]sy, because it was given to the Gentiles, who would trample the holy city for forty-two months. <note>This refers to Revelation 11:2, where the outer court of the temple is abandoned to the non-believers for a period of three and a half years.</note>

What is to be thought of the "voices from heaven," which were then in fashion among the Jews, is well known. Although it cannot well be denied that a divine power dwells within man, which at times speaks very loudly and presents him partly with distant objects and partly with future events that concern him directly—in short, that many a person sometimes has premonitions—the superstitious Jews nevertheless tended to exaggerate the matter far too much. They took all the "speaking ideas" that suddenly awoke in their over-excited imaginations for voices from heaven. Among Christians, these are not infrequently regarded as the workings of the Holy Spirit, especially among those whom an awakened conscience torments because of evil deeds. The Greeks called them warning voices of <term>daemons: in the ancient Greek sense, these were guiding spirits or divine inner voices, such as the "daimonion" described by Socrates, not the modern concept of evil demons</term>.

Our author seems to have learned the "eating of books" from <term>Ezekiel: an Old Testament prophet whose book contains many of the strange symbols—such as flying chariots and scrolls—that influenced the author of Revelation</term>, who likewise ate a parchment scroll written with lamentations, sighing, and woe; it tasted sweet as honey in his mouth, but caused him no stomach cramps, presumably because it was not too large and consequently not difficult to digest. The little book, however, which our author swallowed, and in which the fates of the world or of mankind were determined astrologically—perhaps as in the <term>Key of Solomon: original: "Claviculis Salomonis," a famous medieval grimoire or book of magic and planetary signs attributed to King Solomon</term>—undoubtedly consisted of at least several rolled-up calfskins, which would certainly cause "stomach-pinchers" for any of us if we were to eat some. But joking aside! This whole business of eating books was merely an exaggerated Oriental metaphor, which meant nothing more than that one had read a book with great desire...

### Third Continuation of the Third Vision

...read, or rather, even devoured. The sweet taste indicated the pleasure felt while reading it, whereas the resulting stomach cramps signified the difficulties one had to overcome to digest the teachings contained therein, or to extract <note>The original German uses "heraus zu spündisiren," a colorful term meaning to "tap out" a hidden meaning as one would draw liquid from a barrel's bunghole.</note> the deeply hidden meaning of the <term>hieroglyphs: here meaning mysterious or symbolic signs that require decoding, rather than specifically Egyptian writing</term> with which these books were usually inscribed. In this way, the little book must certainly have tasted sweet to the author: for the <term>art of prophecy: the practice of predicting the future, which the author views here as a personal obsession</term> was his hobby-horse, which seems to have amused him immensely with its whimsical capers, and at the same time gave him the reputation of a great prophet—figures who were then held in general esteem. However, it gave him stomach pains because he viewed the reports and images contained therein—of the periodic revolutions of the stars, their <term>heliacal rising: the moment a star first becomes visible in the eastern sky at dawn, just before being obscured by sunlight</term> or setting, and other natural phenomena—as representations of future events. He may well have often sweated over deciphering these and spent many a restless night.

Now the angel called upon him to prophesy once more to many nations and kings, and this confirms the above suspicion: namely, that he had already finished his <term>apocalyptic drama: the author's skeptical way of referring to the Book of Revelation as a staged literary performance</term> once, and was now apparently intending to perform it a second time in a different manner. Perhaps this second prophecy was a particular inner compulsion of the soul, brought about by the devoured little book; for this probably contained the very important secrets he is now describing.

Regarding the <term>Holy City: a recurring symbol in biblical prophecy, often interpreted as a literal city or a spiritual state</term>, it should be noted here preliminarily that the earliest <term>stargazers: ancient observers of the heavens who blended astronomy and astrology</term> actually understood the <term>Zodiac: the belt of the heavens containing the twelve signs through which the sun and planets appear to move</term> by it. But in later times, real cities certainly also received such...

<meta>This page continues the author’s rationalist critique of the Book of Revelation. Having previously discussed the "eating of the little book," the author now moves to interpret the "holy city" not as a literal location, but as an astronomical and syncretic symbol borrowed from various ancient religious traditions.</meta>

# Revelation 10:8 — 13

heavenly names, such as, for example, *Heliopolis* <note>original: "Heliopolis," Greek for "City of the Sun," an ancient Egyptian city</note>, *Diospolis* <note>original: "Diospolis," Greek for "City of Zeus/Jupiter," a name given to several cities including Thebes</note>, *Jerusalem*, and so on. Perhaps one might object that the holy city in the *Apocalypse* <note>The Book of Revelation</note> is not the <term>Zodiac: the belt of the heavens containing the twelve signs through which the sun, moon, and planets appear to move</term> because the pagans could not "trample" it. However, one must only consider that the author, as mentioned, threw together all the mystical images he could seize upon, whether they belonged to this or another nation: and one will very soon decipher the riddle of the pagans who were to trample this holy city.

It is well known that the ancient <term>mysteries: secret religious rites and initiations of the ancient world</term> were mainly of three kinds. One worshipped there either the stars, or fire, or the one true God. The mysteries of the Egyptians were of the first kind, as is evident from many historical facts that I do not need to cite here. Of the second kind, however, were not only the mysteries of the ancient <term>Magi: the priestly class of ancient Persia and Babylon</term> at *Babylon*, but also the Mithraic mysteries of the Persians. Finally, the great <term>Eleusinian Mysteries: the famous initiation ceremonies held annually for the cult of Demeter and Persephone in ancient Greece</term> were of the third kind.

*Pythagoras*, who had been initiated into most of these mysteries during his travels and well knew that the images he saw were symbols of the workings of nature, took the best from all of them and created new mysteries from them. In these, he likewise taught the existence of a single highest being, which he sometimes called the <term>Monad: from the Greek "Monas," referring to the number one, unity, or the first principle of all things</term>, or the universal sphere of light; sometimes the <term>Triad: from the Greek "Trias," the number three</term>, or spirit, soul, and body; and sometimes the <term>Tetrad: from the Greek "Tetras," the number four</term>, or fire, air, water, and earth. For he quite rightly regarded the universal force of nature as the highest being, and created his *Triad* from *Osiris*, *Isis*, and *Horus*—or from the sun, moon, and generative nature—while his *Tetrad*, as mentioned, was formed from fire, water, air, and earth. ### Third Continuation of the Third Vision

...things his <note>Referring to Pythagoras, mentioned on the previous page</note> disciples designated with the following symbol, in which the **Tetras** <term>Tetras: the number four, often representing the material world or the four elements</term>, **Trias** <term>Trias: the number three, representing the spiritual triad or trinity</term>, and **Monas** <term>Monas: the Monad, or the number one, representing the ultimate unity and divine source of all things</term>, or all of nature, were contained.

<image-desc>A mystical circular diagram representing the "sphere of light." It features a central hexagram composed of two interlaced equilateral triangles. From the circle's edge, numerous fine lines radiate outward like sunbeams. In the very center of the hexagram is a bold, dark, L-shaped or hook-shaped character resembling a Greek Gamma or a stylized Hebrew letter.</image-desc>

Here, the two interleaved equilateral triangles signify the four elements, or the **Tetras**, upon which the sphere of light—the **Monas**, the world-soul, the universal force of nature—continually acts in order to constantly form new products of nature. Specifically, the triangle with one point facing upward is the sign for **Fire**. The other, however, with the point facing downward, signifies **Water**. If one simply removes the two sides <note>The author uses "Schenkel," referring to the legs or sides of the triangle</note> of the latter: then the hieroglyph for **Air** remains. Finally, if one strikes out only the sides of the former: the mark of the **Earth element** is left standing.

### Revelation 10:8–13

<image-desc>
A large, faint geometric diagram is visible in the background, layered behind the printed text. It features two interlaced triangles, one pointing up and one pointing down, forming a hexagram (Seal of Solomon) surrounded by numerous radiating lines. In the center of the figure, a small character is faintly discernible, likely the "Gamma" mentioned in the text.

These four symbols of the four elements still appear in chemical <note>In this historical context, "chemical" refers to alchemy and early chemistry.</note> writings today, and look like this: $\triangle$ $\nabla$ 🜁 🜃. Fire was also called the **Agent** <note>original Latin: *Agens*, the active or driving force</note>, while water was called the **Patient** <note>original Latin: *Patiens*, the passive or receiving element</note>. Therefore, this emblem contained not only the numbers 1, 3, and 4, but also 1, 2, 3, and 4, and consequently seemed very mysterious to many.

The two triangles were chosen as symbols of the **Tetrad** <term>Tetras: the number four, representing the material world</term> because all things are generated or born from the four elements, and because in every generation, Father, Mother, and Child—which are most fittingly represented by this three-sided geometric figure—must always be taken into consideration. Father, Mother, and Child, or Spirit, Soul, and Body, thus made up the **Triad** <term>Trias: the number three, representing spiritual completeness</term> and appeared in the image of a triangle.

The converging rays, which flow from all around onto these two interlaced triangles, signify the **Monad** <term>Monas: the number one, representing ultimate unity or the Divine</term>, the sphere of light, the world-soul, the universal force of nature that animates the elements, forms all kinds of natural products from them, and is therefore intended to be God.

But the **Gamma** <note>The Greek letter Γ, which is used here as a symbol for Earth or measurement</note>, which stands in the middle of the elements, is called **Geometry**, because all things generated from the elements rely on number and measure—that is, on **Geometry**.

Since the most ancient stargazers had necessarily been obliged to divide the heavens from East to West into twelve roughly equal parts, because of the twelve lunar revolutions that made up the lunar year: the Egyptians, Chaldeans, and Brahmins—from whom **Pythagoras** undoubtedly learned his hieroglyphs <note>Here used to mean "sacred symbols"</note> in the mysteries <note>Secret religious or philosophical rites of antiquity</note>—taught that the entire heavens, which they certainly considered a regular hollow vault, were composed of twelve equal regular surfaces, and were consequently a **Dodecahedron**, whose entire surface, as is well known, consists of twelve regular pentagons. ### Third Continuation of the Third Vision

His disciples designated these things with the following symbol, which contained the **Tetrad**, the **Triad**, and the **Monad**—or the entirety of Nature.

<image-desc>A circular diagram with rays emanating from the center. In the middle is a hexagram (the Seal of Solomon), consisting of two interlaced equilateral triangles. In the center of the hexagram, a character is depicted that resembles the Greek letter Gamma (Γ).</image-desc>

Here, the two interlaced equilateral triangles signify the four elements, or the **Tetrad** <term>Tetras: the number four, representing the material world and the four classical elements</term>, upon which the sphere of light, the **Monad** <term>Monas: the number one, representing divine unity or the primal source</term>, the World Soul <note>original: *Weltseele*; the spiritual essence believed to animate the physical universe.</note>, and the universal force of nature constantly act to continuously form new products of nature. The triangle that points its vertex upward is the sign for **Fire**. The other, however, which points its vertex downward, signifies **Water**. If one simply removes the two sides of the latter, the hieroglyph <note>The author uses "hieroglyph" here to mean a sacred or symbolic character, as was common in alchemical and mystical traditions.</note> for **Air** remains. Finally, if one strikes out the sides of the former, the mark of the **Earth** element is left standing.

# Revelation 10:8–13

These four signs of the four elements still appear in chemical <note>By "chemical," the author refers to what we now call alchemical texts.</note> writings today and look like this: $\triangle$ $\nabla$ 
<image-desc>Alchemy symbol for Air: an upright triangle with a horizontal bar through its middle.</image-desc> <image-desc>Alchemy symbol for Earth: an inverted triangle with a horizontal bar through its middle.</image-desc> Fire was also called the **Agent** <note>original: *Agens*; the active or acting principle.</note>, while water was called the **Patient** <note>original: *Patiens*; the passive or receiving principle.</note>. Therefore, this emblem contained not only the numbers 1, 3, and 4, but also 1, 2, 3, and 4, and thus seemed very mysterious to many.

The two triangles were chosen as symbols of the **Tetrad** <term>Tetras: the number four, representing the material elements</term> because all things are sired or born from the four elements; and because in every generation, **Father**, **Mother**, and **Child**—which can most fittingly be represented by this three-sided geometric figure—always come into consideration. **Father**, **Mother**, and **Child**, or **Spirit**, **Soul**, and **Body**, thus made up the **Triad** <term>Trias: the number three, representing a complete spiritual or family unit</term>, and appeared in the image of a triangle.

The converging rays that flow inward from all around onto these two interlaced triangles signify the **Monad** <term>Monas: the divine unity or "The One," the source of all things</term>, the sphere of light, the World Soul <note>original: *Weltseele*</note>, the universal force of nature that animates the elements, forms all kinds of natural products from them, and is therefore intended to be God.

But the **Gamma** <note>The Greek letter Γ</note>, which stands in the middle of the elements, means **Geometry**, because all things sired from the elements are based on number and measure—that is, on geometry.

Since the most ancient stargazers had to divide the heavens from East to West into twelve roughly equal parts due to the twelve lunar revolutions that made up the lunar year, the Egyptians, Chaldeans, and Brahmins—from whom **Pythagoras** undoubtedly learned his hieroglyphs <note>The author uses "hieroglyphs" to refer to the sacred symbolic language of the ancient mystery schools.</note> in the mysteries—taught that the entire sky (which they certainly considered a regular hollow vault) was composed of twelve equal regular surfaces.
